Each state has a department of insurance (DOI), typically led by a commissioner, responsible for regulating all types of insurance (auto, health, home, life, etc.) and protecting consumers.

When informal appeals with the insurer fail, a formal complaint can be escalated to your state’s insurance department. Auto insurance complaints are particularly common, accounting for 35% of all insurance complaints in 2025. The complaint process involves seven steps:

1. Contact your state’s DOI.
2. Gather supporting documents.
3. Write a clear, fact-based complaint letter.
4. Submit your complaint through the specified channels (online, fax, mail).
5. Receive a confirmation and reference number.
6. Await a response or resolution—companies typically respond within 7-30 business days.
7. Follow up if needed.

The DOI investigates the issue and communicates the insurer’s response and the outcome. Most complaints (about 69%) are resolved with some correction, while a small percentage support the insurer’s original decision. Complaint data help regulators monitor insurers' conduct, improving consumer protection overall.</span></p></div></button></div></div></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-4"><div class="flex items-center justify-between mb-0"><a href="https://www.reuters.com/legal/government/teslas-auto-insurance-arm-hit-with-consumer-class-action-california-2025-07-17/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" class="mb-0 flex w-fit items-center gap-2 rounded-full p-1 pl-0"><img width="32" height="32" loading="lazy" alt="reuters.com" class="h-4 w-4 rounded h-[18px] w-[18px] flex-shrink-0 rounded-full" src="https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?domain_url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.reuters.com&amp;sz=32"><div class="mr-2 flex min-w-0 flex-col"><span class="text-token-text-secondary truncate text-[11px] font-medium hover:underline">reuters.com</span></div></a></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-1"><div class="flex min-w-0 flex-col"><p class="text-token-text-primary text-opacity-80 truncate text-sm text-[14px] leading-[18px] font-[590] tracking-[-0.3px]">Tesla's auto insurance arm hit with consumer class action in California</p></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-2"><button type="button" class="hover:bg-token-bg-elevated-primary flex w-full cursor-pointer items-start text-left" aria-label="Open scanned source Tesla's auto insurance arm hit with consumer class action in California"><div class="flex flex-1 overflow-hidden"><p class="text-token-text-secondary line-clamp-2 p-0 text-xs break-words whitespace-normal"><span class="text-token-text-secondary inline text-[14px] leading-[18px] tracking-[-0.3px] [overflow-wrap:anywhere] break-words hover:underline">Tesla's auto insurance division is facing a proposed consumer class action lawsuit in California, accusing the company of systematically delaying and minimizing insurance payouts. Filed on July 11 in the Los Angeles Superior Court, the suit claims Tesla Insurance failed to maintain adequate claims-handling procedures, leading many policyholders to abandon their claims or cover costs personally. The legal action invokes California’s unfair competition law and seeks to represent potentially thousands of customers.

Tesla, which began offering insurance in 2019, markets it as a superior alternative to traditional providers. However, both customer complaints and regulatory scrutiny have emerged, including findings by the Ohio Department of Insurance in 2023 that Tesla failed to comply with claims processing rules. The named plaintiff, who insured a leased Dodge Ram through Tesla, allegedly received no compensation after the vehicle was stolen, experiencing significant delays and lack of communication from Tesla. The lawsuit seeks unspecified monetary damages.

The case, Carlos Magana v. Tesla Insurance Co (No. 25STCV20622), is still in preliminary stages, with Tesla yet to respond. Attorneys Maro Burunsuzyan and David Scott represent the plaintiff.</span></p></div></button></div></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-1"><div class="flex min-w-0 flex-col"><p class="text-token-text-primary text-opacity-80 truncate text-sm text-[14px] leading-[18px] font-[590] tracking-[-0.3px]">US issues urgent safety warning after two drivers killed in crashes with faulty air bags</p></div><div class="flex flex-col gap-2"><button type="button" class="hover:bg-token-bg-elevated-primary flex w-full cursor-pointer items-start text-left" aria-label="Open scanned source US issues urgent safety warning after two drivers killed in crashes with faulty air bags"><div class="flex flex-1 overflow-hidden"><p class="text-token-text-secondary line-clamp-2 p-0 text-xs break-words whitespace-normal"><span class="text-token-text-secondary inline text-[14px] leading-[18px] tracking-[-0.3px] [overflow-wrap:anywhere] break-words hover:underline">The U.S. National Highway Traffic Safety Administration (NHTSA) has issued an urgent safety warning following two recent driver deaths caused by faulty air bag inflators made in China by Jilin Province Detiannuo Automobile Safety System Co., Ltd. (DTN). These inflators, likely illegally imported, were installed as replacements in used vehicles, often after previous crashes. NHTSA has identified 10 total incidents—eight resulting in death and two causing severe injuries—linked to these substandard inflators, which shoot dangerous metal fragments into vehicle occupants upon deployment. The affected vehicles in the fatal cases were Chevrolet Malibus and Hyundai Sonatas, though the risk may not be limited to these brands. NHTSA has launched an investigation and is collaborating with law enforcement to curb illegal imports. Auto shops are urged to inspect replacement inflators and alert NHTSA if DTN products are found. Consumers buying used cars previously involved in accidents are advised to verify the authenticity and safety of any replacement air bags.</span></p></div></button></div></div></div><div class="flex The 73.9 ft from the PNG looks important too.</p></div></div></div></div><div class="text-token-text-secondary flex items-stretch gap-1"><div class="flex w-6 shrink-0 flex-col items-center"><div class="text-token-text-secondary mt-0 flex h-5 w-5 items-center justify-center"><div class="bg-token-text-secondary h-1.5 w-1.5 rounded-full"></div></div><div class="bg-token-border-light mt-2 w-px flex-1"></div></div><div class="flex-1"><div class=""><div class="text-token-text-primary text-[14px] leading-5">Estimating minimal repair needs for roadworthiness</div><div class="text-token-text-secondary mt-2 space-y-2 text-sm leading-5"><p class="break-words whitespace-pre-wrap">I’m considering whether to estimate minimal repairs.
